# Don's F1 Addiction

A React / TypeScript frontend and FastAPI backend for championship context,
empirical weekend comparisons, recorded lap replay and auditable race predictions.

## Pages

| Route | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `/` | Driver / constructor standings, previous-round movement, animated schematic championship order, F1 calendar and next session in the viewer's timezone |
| `/weekend` | Same-segment qualifying and matched-lap teammate race comparisons, sample counts, exploratory intervals and tyre-age trends |
| `/prediction` | Version descriptions, saved winner distributions, post-race Brier / log-loss scores and trained-iteration validation metrics |
| `/ghost-car` | Two recorded fastest laps with play / pause / scrub controls and speed, throttle, brake, gear, DRS and cumulative time traces |
| `/looking-back` | Cited W11 / RB19 / MCL39 engineering context, conceptual car diagrams, signed qualifying advantage and recorded lap comparisons |
| `/not-just-f1` | Mixed-series calendar: F1, WEC / Hypercar, IMSA, all ten 2026 NLS races, GT Europe and Supercars; browser-local calendar imports |
| `/best-car` | Redirect to `/looking-back` for existing bookmarks |

See [the refactor notes](docs/F1_REFACTOR.md) for the original findings, methodology,
limits, source provenance, model workflow and verification details.

## Install and run

Python 3.10+ and Node.js 18+ are required. Activate your existing Python virtual
environment first. Run these commands from the repository root:

```bash
python -m pip install -r backend/requirements.txt
npm --prefix frontend install
npm --prefix frontend run build
python -m uvicorn backend.app:app --reload --port 8000
```

In another terminal:

```bash
npm --prefix frontend run dev
```

Open `http://localhost:5173`. API documentation is at `http://localhost:8000/docs`.
The Vite development proxy forwards `/api` to the backend. A production host must
serve the React assets and forward `/api` to FastAPI; `vite preview` alone does not
provide this backend proxy.

No F1TV account is needed for historical analysis from 2023 onwards. The backend
uses existing FastF1 caches and free OpenF1 historical timing, with Jolpica for
published classifications. See [Backend data loading](docs/F1_DATA_LOADING.md)
for setup, diagnostics, coverage and optional archive access.

The uploaded code export excluded lockfiles, so this patch deliberately leaves
your existing `frontend/package-lock.json` untouched. `npm install` updates it;
commit the resulting lockfile in your repository after reviewing it.

## Runtime data

`var/` holds SQLite prediction history and JSON model iterations. The backend
reuses populated legacy FastF1 caches when available, otherwise `var/fastf1`.
Set `F1_CACHE_DIR` to select the cache explicitly. Set `F1_DATA_DIR` to a persistent absolute path when deploying. Keep
this directory out of version control (add `/var/` to your repository's existing
`.gitignore`). Existing CLI tools and their caches are retained; the web backend
shares the selected cache. OpenF1 responses and its shared rate budget are stored
in `var/openf1/openf1.sqlite3`. First uncached loads need network access and can
take time; subsequent requests reuse the cache. Set `F1_TIMING_PROVIDER=openf1`
to force the free provider, or `fastf1` for optional native archive access.
The default `auto` prefers existing timing caches, then OpenF1.

Season analyses run in a bounded background queue and report their progress. Run
one Uvicorn worker with this in-process job queue; multiple independent workers
need a shared job store / queue before deployment. Forecast snapshots are durable
SQLite records, independently of those transient analysis jobs.

## Verify

```bash
python -m unittest discover -s backend/tests -v
npm --prefix frontend test
npm --prefix frontend run build
```

Frontend interaction tests use DOM fixtures, not live data or a rendered browser.
The offline backend suite exercises model adapters, forecast persistence / scoring,
chronological training, lap matching and timezone conversion.

## Create an empirical model iteration

Legacy v1–v12 remain available with explicit limitations. They are not relabelled
as empirically validated models. v13 uses learned numeric features without driver
names, team names or personal tier assignments.

```bash
python -m backend.train_predictor --from-year 2023 --through-year 2025
```

After a later race, create a new immutable iteration, for example:

```bash
python -m backend.train_predictor --from-year 2023 --through-year 2026 --through-round 10
```

Use the last round with completed, published results. Reload the Predictor page
to see the new iteration. Training downloads historical sessions and needs at
least 15 complete events. No trained v13 weights or claimed accuracy are bundled.
Models trained after a selected weekend cannot forecast that weekend through the
API. Older eligible iterations remain selectable.

## Other motorsport calendars

F1 sessions refresh from FastF1. Other series ship with a source-labelled 2026
calendar snapshot checked on 7 November 2026. These are mostly event dates, not
complete timed session schedules. Two published Monza WEC session starts are
included. Exact timed entries are converted using the viewer's timezone and its
DST rules. Date-only weekends remain venue dates.

On “Not Just F1”, import a downloaded publisher `.ics` file to replace the selected
series / year in your browser. Your imported data stays in that browser. For a
shared repository snapshot:

```bash
python -m backend.services.ical path/to/publisher-calendar.ics \
  --series imsa --year 2027 --source-url https://www.imsa.com/schedule
```

Inspect and commit `data/motorsport-2027.json`. Imports require UTC or an explicit
timezone, reject unexpanded recurring entries, omit cancelled entries and replace
the chosen series / year so removed sessions do not survive a refresh. There is
no automatic HTML scraping or invented session time for unsupported providers.

## Original standalone tools

The `formula1-prediction`, `formula1-ghost-car` and `formula1-best-car` tools remain.
Install their own requirements to use their desktop / CLI export functionality.
The website replays fastest laps; the standalone full-race replay is not exposed
as a web feature in this patch.


============================================================
FILE: ./docs/F1_DATA_LOADING.md
============================================================
# Historical data without an F1TV account

## Pending sessions in the frontend

HTTP 425 means the selected session has not started, its results are not yet
published, or its free historical data is still pending. The UI shows an
informational status with the provider's explanation and suggests selecting an
earlier completed session. For GET requests, **Check again** retries manually;
when `Retry-After` is supplied, its earliest retry time appears in the user's
local time and the button stays disabled until then. This is not a promise of
publication at that time. The frontend never polls automatically. Changing the
selection or leaving the page clears the previous notice and cancels its timer.
Prediction submissions use the same status notice; **Run & save** remains the
explicit action. Other HTTP errors retain their error styling. HTTP 425 entries
in the backend access log remain expected; no results are fabricated to hide them.

This patch applies after `f1-backend-data-loading-fix.patch`. No F1TV account,
API key, login, or environment variable is required for the default historical
route. Install requirements and restart the backend:

```bash
python -m pip install -r backend/requirements.txt
python -m uvicorn backend.app:app --reload --port 8000
```

## Provider selection

| `F1_TIMING_PROVIDER` | Behaviour |
| --- | --- |
| omitted / `auto` | Existing usable FastF1 cache, then public OpenF1 historical timing. Optional authenticated archive fallback only when credentials already exist. |
| `openf1` | Use public OpenF1 historical timing directly; no archive fallback. |
| `fastf1` | Previous native cache/archive behaviour; uncached protected data requires eligible archive access. |

Published Q/R/Sprint classifications, standings and result scoring continue to
use Jolpica. Public schedule resolution tries the FastF1 schedule repository and
Jolpica, never the protected timing backend. The OpenF1 session is matched by
session name and a unique UTC start within 12 hours, not by assuming its meeting
key equals an F1 round. Cancelled, future, ambiguous and live sessions return
specific errors. A delayed session's observed finish extends the historical
waiting window. The source's actual availability still governs every channel.

OpenF1 history starts in 2023. Free access excludes the live window (until 30
minutes after a session ends). Earlier timing needs an existing cache or optional
native access. W11-era published qualifying charts still use Jolpica; this patch
does not invent 2020 telemetry.

## Empirical analysis and replay

OpenF1 laps are mapped into the existing backend schema: lap and sector times,
roster, compound, stint, completed-lap tyre age, pit exclusions, weather and
race-control screening. Known stale race-control messages outside the session
window are removed. Reported deleted laps use the affected lap in message text,
not the leader's lap at message publication. An unassignable deletion fails
closed. Overlapping stint ranges and duplicate lap identities are excluded.

`IsAccurate` remains unknown and `TrackStatus` remains `UNKNOWN`: OpenF1's screen
is not FastF1's accuracy flag. A separate OpenF1 eligibility column requires
consistent positive sectors, known pit coverage, no opening/pit/reported-deleted
lap, and a whole-lap interval without outstanding race-control hazards. Missing
coverage is excluded. Sector CLEAR cannot end SC/VSC or a red flag; uncertain
restarts remain excluded until an explicit track GREEN. This intentionally loses
some usable laps. Unreported deletions/incidents, approximate timestamps, traffic,
fuel and setup remain limitations. Sources, counts and warnings are returned and
shown on Weekend by Weekend and Ghost Car. Qualifying gaps use published shared
Q1/Q2/Q3 results; phases are never guessed from OpenF1 lap timing.

Replay fetches only the requested driver and selected lap's time window from
`car_data` and `location`. It matches position within 0.5 seconds, rejects long
sample gaps, normalizes brake to 0/1 and integrates speed for estimated distance.
Recorded positions are approximate (~3.7 Hz); replay and distance-aligned time
deltas are illustrative, not precise engineering measurements. Missing gear, speed or location is reported rather than filled with fabricated
channels. Missing DRS (notably the 2026 aero feed) remains null and plots as gaps;
it does not discard otherwise usable car/position samples.

The web practice-model adapters and v13 feature extraction accept these sessions.
V13 input metadata and training manifests include source provenance. Train a new
iteration to use newly available practice evidence; existing model artifacts are
not silently rewritten. Legacy CLI entry points still use their original native
FastF1 loaders; this patch changes the web backend and its training command.

## Cache, limits and diagnostics

OpenF1 JSON responses live in `$F1_DATA_DIR/openf1/openf1.sqlite3` (default
`var/openf1/openf1.sqlite3`). Successful non-empty historical responses are cached
for seven days, session metadata for one hour, and empty responses for at most
60 seconds. SQLite shares a conservative 2.1-second request spacing across
workers on this data directory. HTTP 429 sets shared backoff; cached responses
remain readable. The budget cannot coordinate other applications or machines
sharing your public IP. Keep this cache directory persistent and out of Git.

No full-session high-frequency download, blanket cache clearing, or automatic
retry loop is performed. Optional missing channels give warnings; failed required
channels return typed 425/502/503 responses and `Retry-After` when applicable.
An uncached weekend can take tens of seconds. Production proxies should allow
sufficient request time; repeated refreshes consume the same shared budget.

```bash
python -m backend.check_data --year 2026 --round 19 --session R --timing
python -m backend.check_data --year 2026 --round 19 --session Q --timing --telemetry-driver VER
curl http://localhost:8000/api/data/status
```

`authentication.authenticated: false` is expected without F1TV. Check the actual
`timing.source` and any provider warnings. Set `F1_TIMING_PROVIDER=openf1` in the
backend process environment to force the public provider when diagnosing.

Sources: [OpenF1 API documentation](https://openf1.org/docs/) and
[access, historical window and limits](https://openf1.org/).

## Verification of the public provider patch

Backend regressions cover source selection without archive auth, public-only
metadata, published results, teammate matching, missing channels, ambiguous and
live sessions, deletion/pit/hazard screening, duplicate laps, approximate
telemetry matching, missing 2026 DRS, disk cache reuse and shared rate backoff.
Frontend checks compile TypeScript, build production assets and exercise all six
routes in the DOM, including source warnings and absent DRS. Browser rendering
and authenticated archive access are not claimed as tested here.

Live checks without credentials loaded 1,360 Mexico 2026 race laps, conservatively
retained 62 pace samples, and returned a successful VER/HAM replay comparison
with 298 samples per driver and null DRS. These counts describe one provider
snapshot, not guaranteed coverage for every session.

## Previous native archive follow-up (optional mode)

The following notes describe `F1_TIMING_PROVIDER=fastf1`; the default public route
above supersedes any statement below that raw timing always needs F1TV.

## Cause of the reported failures

FastF1 can return a driver/results list even when timing downloads fail. A
“Finished loading data for 22 drivers” log is not evidence that lap timing,
weather, race control or car position loaded. The previous shared loader then
turned missing laps into a generic ValueError, which routes labelled 404 / 422.

There are two relevant upstream changes:

- FastF1 3.9 added authenticated archive access after Formula 1 required login in
  October 2026. An active F1TV Access/Pro/Premium account is required for uncached
  protected timing downloads.
- FastF1 3.9.1 / 3.9.2 fixed the unexpected timestamp format in the 2026 Mexican
  Grand Prix archive, including telemetry. The backend now requires 3.9.2 and
  stays within the tested 3.9 release series.

Sources: [maintainer release notes](https://github.com/theOehrly/Fast-F1/releases)
and [authentication documentation](https://github.com/theOehrly/Fast-F1/blob/main/docs/api_reference/accounts_auth.rst).

## Optional native archive installation and authentication

Activate the same Python environment used by the backend, stop the server and run:

```bash
python -m pip install -r backend/requirements.txt
python -c "import fastf1; print(fastf1.__version__)"
python -m fastf1 auth f1tv --authenticate
python -m uvicorn backend.app:app --reload --port 8000
```

Enter account credentials in the FastF1 terminal prompt. The backend reuses the
locally stored token from the same OS user. A free Formula 1 account without an
eligible subscription does not grant protected archive access. Tokens expire;
authenticate again when asked. If direct sign-in fails, FastF1 also supports:

```bash
python -m fastf1 auth f1tv --authenticate --method browser
```

Follow FastF1's browser/addon instructions. There is no backend login form, no
hardcoded token and no account credential included in this patch. For unattended
native renewal, FastF1 supports `F1_USERNAME` / `F1_PASSWORD` in the server process
environment. Never put their values into a tracked file or a support log.

The server will not prompt for a password or open a browser inside an API worker.
The small internal FastF1 auth bridge and cache-existence adapter are isolated in
`backend/services/f1_access.py`, covered by tests, and constrained to FastF1 3.9.x.

## What works without protected timing access

Published Q / R / Sprint classifications are fetched directly through Jolpica,
without calling `Session.load()` on the protected F1 streams. These support driver
lists, qualifying-anchor legacy models, result scoring, era qualifying charts and
the qualifying part of Weekend by Weekend. Their source is explicitly recorded.

A partial weekend response includes qualifying teammate gaps from the deepest
shared published Q segment, warnings and `dataSources`. Race gaps, tyre trends,
weather and matched-lap counts are not fabricated when raw race timing is absent.

Practice-based predictions and detailed race/telemetry analysis require session data, now supplied by OpenF1 by default.
The empirical model uses its existing missing-practice indicator if raw practice
cannot be loaded; its input metadata states that omission. Training outcome labels
now use published race classifications, with a conservative next-day availability
cutoff when the exact publication time is unknown. No future result is used in a
prediction's features.

## Cache handling

Existing caches from `f1_cache/`, `formula1-prediction/f1_cache/`,
`formula1-ghost-car/.fastf1_cache/` and `formula1-best-car/cache/` are discovered.
A populated existing parsed cache is preferred; otherwise `var/fastf1` is used.
Missing parsed files from other known locations are copied without overwriting
or deleting originals. `F1_CACHE_DIR` explicitly selects the cache location;
`F1_DATA_DIR` controls prediction history, model storage and the separate OpenF1 cache.

There is no blanket cache clearing and no `ignore_version=True`. FastF1's cache
version checks still apply. Cache-only attempts are request-context scoped rather
than changing global offline mode for other threads. An entirely cold session is
not passed through every parser just to produce repeated “Failed to load” logs.
Only usable timing sessions enter the in-memory success cache. Short failure
memoization avoids repeated downloads; CLI token-file changes are detected.

Weather and race-control properties can raise DataNotLoadedError instead of
returning an empty DataFrame. They are treated as missing optional channels, with
warnings. Their absence does not discard valid lap timing. Missing race-control
information still limits deleted-lap verification; warnings retain that caveat.
A FastF1 SystemExit on a bad cache update is converted into a data error rather
than terminating the API worker.

## Diagnostics

```bash
python -m backend.check_data --year 2026 --round 19 --session Q
python -m backend.check_data --year 2026 --round 19 --session Q --timing
python -m fastf1 auth f1tv --status
```

The first command verifies public results. The second also verifies raw laps.
The diagnostic prints version, selected cache, counts, warnings and status only;
it does not print a password or token. `GET /api/data/status` provides a minimal
status response without triggering login or archive requests.

Upstream failures carry an `X-F1-Data-Error` header and a readable `detail`:

| Code | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `fastf1_upgrade_required` | Old FastF1 runtime is still loaded; install and restart |
| `archive_auth_required` | No usable cached timing and no valid login |
| `archive_auth_rejected` | Archive rejected authentication; log in again |
| `archive_access_denied` | Upstream HTTP 403; check entitlement / network |
| `archive_network_error` | Archive connection failed |
| `session_not_started` / `session_not_published` | Timing is not available yet (425) |
| `archive_rate_limited` | Provider requested a pause |
| `timing_data_incomplete` | Archive responded, but required timing channels failed |

Archive authentication is a server-side data dependency, so its failure is 503,
not an app-login 401 or a missing-route 404. Invalid model choices remain 422.
Scoring uses current published classifications and preserves genuine provider
errors rather than reporting every failure as “race not finished”.

## Verification

```bash
python -m unittest discover -s backend/tests -v
```

Regression tests cover cold/partial/warm caches, native auth without prompts,
HTTP failure classification, cache migration, timezone-safe result loading,
public qualifying fallback and the existing model/analysis contracts.

Live checks from this execution environment loaded 22 classified drivers and a
normalized v12 Q forecast for Mexico 2026, plus 11 published teammate Q comparisons.
No F1TV account was available here, so authenticated raw lap/position downloads
are not claimed as verified. The diagnostic / auth commands above verify that
last dependency on the machine running the backend.

============================================================
# F1 evidence dashboard: implementation and review notes

This patch is based on the supplied `f1-addiction-llm.txt` source snapshot. It adds
no invented fuel measurements, driver preference tiers to the empirical model,
or claimed component-specific lap-time gains.

## Original review findings and resolutions

| Finding | Resolution / remaining limitation |
| --- | --- |
| Alphabetical discovery defaulted to v9 instead of v12 | Numeric discovery; exact model IDs; latest trained iteration is explicitly selected when available |
| v4–v9 constructor mismatch | Shared adapter supplies physics, grid and GP arguments correctly |
| v12 returned a tuple while API expected a dictionary | Winner and margin outputs unpacked separately |
| NumPy timedelta `.values[0].total_seconds()` silently discarded qualifying times | Scalar extraction uses `.iloc[0]` in v10–v12; regression tested |
| Empty v1–v3 physics profiles bypassed practice analysis | Available completed practice at or before the selected input is analysed; missing evidence is labelled as a grid fallback |
| v1 fallback weights were divided by configured simulation count | Normalize actual output weights / counts, preserve zero-probability drivers, reject invalid distributions |
| v5 had a broken `TrackConfig.get_laps.get` call | Uses the existing lap-count dictionary |
| v10 / v11 truncated a 22-car field to 20 | Include the supplied field; v12 retains its 22-car implementation |
| Missing `rich` backend dependency | Added with calendar parsing and test HTTP dependencies |
| Ghost website did not show available channels or replay | Added actual-position replay, throttle, binary brake, gear, raw DRS and cumulative time delta |
| Speed difference could be confused with time gained | Cumulative time delta is now separately calculated and labelled |
| Best Car showed zero whenever primary team was fastest | Signed primary-vs-nearest-other-team Q1 gap; seconds and percent retained |
| Qualifying gaps mixed Q1 / Q2 / Q3 | Era progression uses common Q1; teammate comparisons use deepest shared timed segment |
| Season load blocked the whole result and hid missing races | Bounded background job, progress polling, explicit missing-race list |
| Per-router global cache reconfiguration | Web routes use one shared loader and cache |
| Frontend swallowed fetch failures and accepted stale selections | Shared abortable request hook, cleared dependent results and visible errors |
| No durable forecast records or honest post-race scoring | Immutable SQLite snapshots and a winner-distribution evaluation endpoint |
| No model validation / version evidence in frontend | Catalog describes legacy changes; trained iterations report held-out metrics and baselines |
| Hardcoded frontend year limits and default 2025 | Season service supplies years through current year; current year is initial selection |
| Testing support was partially wired | Testing remains excluded from the web session comparison; original CLI testing support remains |

## Empirical teammate analysis

“Same car” is a useful partial control, not an experimental randomization.
Teammates can have different parts, setup, damage, engine modes and instructions.

1. Qualifying: fastest valid lap in the deepest Q segment where both have a time.
   Q1 vs Q3 is never presented as a like-for-like teammate comparison.
2. Race: pair by the same race lap and compound, and require tyre age within one
   lap. This reduces differences in fuel-burn timing and weather timing.
3. Exclude inaccurate, deleted, synthesized, pit-in / out and non-green laps.
   Traffic is not guessed from a slow lap and is not claimed to be removed.
4. Report median A-minus-B time, counts and every retained pair. Negative is A
   faster. Qualifying-vs-race reversal is an observation, not a driver ranking.
5. At eight or more pairs, show an exploratory 95% moving-block bootstrap
   interval using blocks of three within contiguous matched stint segments.
   It is conditional on the retained sample, not a causal confidence statement.
6. Tyre-age trends fit lap time against race lap and tyre age within each driver /
   compound. Require 15 valid laps, two stints and an identifiable, sufficiently
   conditioned design matrix. Otherwise return unavailable. Negative slopes are
   retained rather than clipped into “wear”. These are descriptive pace slopes;
   fuel, weather, traffic and stint effects remain.

The field overview also compares each lap with at least two other drivers on the
same race lap / compound and tyre age within one lap. It reports the median
percentage difference only after five matching laps. Peer composition varies;
this is descriptive performance evidence alongside qualifying / finish position,
not a pure car-performance or driver-skill ranking.

Public data here includes compound, tyre age, lap/sector times, speed, throttle,
binary brake, gear, position and provider DRS codes. It does not contain measured
fuel kilograms, suspension setup, tyre carcass temperature, physical wear, brake
pressure or aerodynamic loads. Brake is an on/off channel, not pressure percent.
Tyre compounds labelled SOFT/MEDIUM/HARD are not identical specifications across
all circuits. Raw DRS codes are not automatically interpreted as 2026 active aero.

## Forecasting and learning

### Legacy adapters

The API loads actual completed input sessions. Earlier-session forecasts never
read later practice or the target race for their features. Qualifying-only v10 /
v11 reject practice inputs. The original CLI models retain their manually tuned
pace, fuel, tyre and driver assumptions. The UI exposes those limitations.

Their existing GP-name lookups are adapted to the names selected by the website.
The sparse original track tables remain assumptions; v1–v3 retain a labelled
58-lap default. A fixed seed makes runs repeatable; global legacy RNG use is
serialized and the prior state restored. This does not turn legacy models into
calibrated empirical probability models.

### v13 learned iterations

Each driver is represented by:

- Relative Q1 gap as a percentage of the fastest Q1 time.
- Qualifying result rank fraction (not a claim to know grid penalties).
- Prior completed practice long-run pace relative to that compound's field
  median, and an indicator for missing practice evidence.

The classifier never receives driver/team identity. Missing qualifying times use
a documented fallback of at least 5% or the slowest observed relative gap. Fuel
load and practice-run intent remain unobserved; relative practice pace is a proxy.

Train whole events chronologically. The first 60% fit candidate regularizations;
the next 20% choose regularization; the final 20% are untouched test events. Score
once on that test split, then refit the selected specification on all included
training events. Test scores describe the pre-refit holdout fit. They are not
future guarantees or scores of a final model already refitted on the test races.

Model output is normalized logistic winner likelihood over the actual field.
Evaluate event-level multiclass Brier (sum of squared errors), log loss and top-1
winner accuracy. Compare the same test races with equal probabilities and a fixed
50%-to-qualifying-leader baseline. No automatic “better” claim follows from a new
version number. A previous model is only compared on this holdout when it was
trained before the holdout began.

Artifacts are immutable JSON numeric coefficients with a data-manifest hash,
training cutoff, event list, split and metrics. Re-running the same manifest
reuses its artifact; changed features / labels create a new ID. The data and
algorithm version participate in the hash. No downloaded pickle is executed.

A training result is not promoted silently over an older iteration. It is added
to the catalog and selectable; the latest iteration is the default for later
weekends. The server rejects it for any event at or before its training cutoff.
For a rolling backtest, train only through round N and predict N+1, retaining
those saved runs. Training needs published historical data; weights are not
fabricated when downloads fail or fewer than 15 events are available.

### Prediction audit and scoring

Saved snapshots include exact probabilities, model ID and source hash, seed,
creation time, event, selected session and training cutoff. Learned runs also
retain their numeric feature entries. A run created after race start is labelled
retrospective even when its features came from qualifying. Legacy historical
runs are not presented as genuine pre-race forecasts. This does not certify the
historical freshness of mutable upstream data or manually authored legacy rules.

Post-race scoring uses the saved probabilities, not a rerun. It requires a
finished session and an available P1 classification. An excluded winner receives
zero probability (log loss clipped at 1e-15). Classification and penalties can
change; the evaluation includes a retrieval time. Winner rank is not a forecast
finishing order. Persistent snapshots survive restart; evaluations refresh from
the current classification.

## Calendar behavior and provenance

F1 timings use FastF1 UTC session timestamps; browser `Intl` formatting uses the
viewer's timezone including DST. The next session is the earliest published
future session start in the selected season; the homepage refreshes every five
minutes. It does not infer session ends or claim that the next session is live.

Standings come from Jolpica through FastF1. Driver and constructor snapshots are
aligned to one published round. Movement is against round N-1, not an arbitrary
seven-day window. Sprint points and published adjustments are included. First
round / newly appearing entries have no prior-position delta.

The top-down championship animation depicts ranks on a schematic oval. Spacing
is not elapsed time or points. The separate recorded lap replay interpolates
FastF1 X/Y samples on a common elapsed-time axis, starting both laps at t=0. It
is not a reconstruction of an on-track battle or precision racing-line tracking.

Other-series data is a checked-in snapshot, not a live scraper. The bundled 2026
snapshot includes all ten NLS races and WEC, IMSA WeatherTech, GT World Challenge
Europe and Supercars event dates. Hypercar is a WEC class rather than another
calendar to duplicate. Each entry retains a source URL and a check date.

- [NLS calendar](https://www.nuerburgring-langstrecken-serie.de/language/en/calendar-nurburgring-langstrecken-serie-2026/)
- [IMSA schedule announcement](https://www.imsa.com/news/2025/march/13/imsa-reveals-2026-weathertech-championship-michelin-pilot-challenge-schedules)
- [GT Europe calendar](https://gt-world-challenge-europe.com/calendar)
- [Supercars revised calendar](https://www.supercars.com/news/supercars-news-2026-change-of-date-penrite-oil-sandown-500-statement-calendar)
- [WEC original schedule](https://www.fiawec.com/en/news/le-calendrier-2026-du-fia-wec-devoile/8356)
- [WEC replacement final rounds](https://www.fiawec.com/en/news/fia-wec-and-fia-confirm-venues-for-final-two-rounds-of-2026/13698)
- [WEC Monza qualifying / race start times](https://www.fiawec.com/en/news/high-speeds-and-high-stakes-in-monza-season-finale/13912)

Date-only entries have exclusive end dates, as iCalendar specifies. They never
become false midnight local sessions. UTC / timezone-aware imported sessions are
converted; floating times are rejected. Browser imports need a VTIMEZONE definition
for non-UTC TZID entries and remain local. The CLI can use recognized IANA TZIDs.
Import replaces a series/year, including deletions; recurrent events need prior
expansion. No server endpoint fetches arbitrary user-submitted URLs.

## Looking Back and engineering evidence

Engineering topics retain publisher links in the API and UI. Source descriptions
are distinct from measured outcomes. W11 suspension / cooling, RB19 floor changes
and MCL39 brake cooling are starting points for investigating traces, not proof
of an isolated lap-time benefit. Concept animations are labelled schematics;
there is no fabricated CFD, temperature map or proprietary car geometry.

Era advantage uses primary **team identity**, including replacement drivers,
rather than assuming one fixed pair raced every round. Compare best Q1 lap to the
fastest other team's Q1 lap. Negative seconds/percent means advantage; missing
rounds are disclosed. Percent gaps improve scale comparisons but do not remove
circuit, weather, rule or tyre differences between years.

## Verification and operational limits

Run the commands in README after applying the patch. The backend suite uses
synthetic data to test the actual adapters and analysis contracts. DOM tests mount
all six routes, navigate tabs, replay championship movement, save / score a
forecast and operate lap controls. They do not verify browser layout or upstream
availability. Production TypeScript / Vite build is also checked.

During development, live F1 schedules and Jolpica standings responded. Raw
FastF1 session timing could not be loaded from this execution environment; the
provider attempted account authentication and timing data remained unavailable.
Consequently full live telemetry, empirical season training and a real historical
accuracy claim are not verified here. No credentials or model weights are bundled.
A browser binary download was also unavailable; UI checks use DOM interaction
fixtures, not a claimed visual browser run.

The season-analysis queue is intentionally single-worker and process-local,
limited to ten retained/queued jobs with one running download worker. Completed
jobs are reused for an hour. A backend restart expires those jobs; the UI offers
an explicit error on a lost job. Use a shared queue before adding server workers.
FastF1 upstream caching remains in addition to a bounded session-object cache.
Network requests are subject to provider availability and rate limits.

## Follow-up: archive access and public result separation

The data-loading follow-up supersedes the original generic missing-data handling.
See [F1_DATA_LOADING.md](F1_DATA_LOADING.md) for the FastF1 3.9.2 requirement,
optional F1TV authentication, preserved caches and public-classification fallback.
The subsequent OpenF1 patch adds free historical timing (2023+), with a separate
conservative quality screen, lazy per-lap telemetry, source warnings, persistent
cache and shared request throttling. No F1TV login is needed for that route.
Qualifying / outcome labels can use published results without protected timing.
Raw practice evidence is still optional and explicitly marked missing.

"""Diagnose public classifications and historical timing without credentials."""

import argparse
import json
import sys

import fastf1
from .services.data import CACHE_DIR, load_session, optional_data
from .services.data import timing_provider
from .services.f1_access import MIN_VERSION, auth_status
from .services.loading_errors import SessionDataError


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument("--year", type=int, required=True)
    parser.add_argument("--round", type=int, required=True)
    parser.add_argument(
        "--session", choices=["FP1", "FP2", "FP3", "Q", "R", "S", "SQ"], default="Q"
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--timing", action="store_true", help="Also verify raw lap timing access"
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--telemetry-driver",
        help="Also verify recorded fastest-lap telemetry for this driver code or number",
    )
    args = parser.parse_args()
    report = {
        "fastf1Version": fastf1.__version__,
        "minimumVersion": MIN_VERSION,
        "cacheDirectory": str(CACHE_DIR),
        "authentication": auth_status(),
        "timingProvider": timing_provider(),
    }
    failed = False
    if args.session in ("Q", "R", "S"):
        try:
            session = load_session(
                args.year, args.round, args.session, results_only=True
            )
            report["classification"] = {
                "source": session.data_source,
                "drivers": len(session.results),
            }
        except SessionDataError as exc:
            report["classification"] = {"code": exc.code, "detail": exc.detail}
            failed = True
    if args.timing or args.telemetry_driver or args.session not in ("Q", "R", "S"):
        try:
            session = load_session(args.year, args.round, args.session)
            report["timing"] = {
                "source": session.data_source,
                "laps": len(session.laps),
                "warnings": session.data_warnings,
            }
            if args.telemetry_driver:
                lap = session.laps.pick_drivers(args.telemetry_driver).pick_fastest()
                if lap is None:
                    raise SessionDataError(
                        "lap_unavailable",
                        "No screened timed lap is available for this driver.",
                        425,
                        60,
                    )
                report["telemetry"] = {
                    "driver": args.telemetry_driver,
                    "samples": len(lap.get_telemetry()),
                }
        except SessionDataError as exc:
            report["timing"] = {"code": exc.code, "detail": exc.detail}
            failed = True
    print(json.dumps(report, indent=2))
    return int(failed)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

"""Immutable prediction snapshots, persisted across backend restarts."""

import json
import sqlite3
import uuid
from .data import DATA_DIR


def connect():
    db = sqlite3.connect(DATA_DIR / "predictions.sqlite3", timeout=30)
    db.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL")
    db.execute(
        "CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS runs (id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, created TEXT NOT NULL, payload TEXT NOT NULL)"
    )
    return db


def save(payload):
    payload = {**payload, "runId": str(uuid.uuid4())}
    with connect() as db:
        db.execute(
            "INSERT INTO runs VALUES (?, ?, ?)",
            (
                payload["runId"],
                payload["createdAt"],
                json.dumps(payload, allow_nan=False),
            ),
        )
    return payload


def get(run_id):
    with connect() as db:
        row = db.execute("SELECT payload FROM runs WHERE id=?", (run_id,)).fetchone()
    return json.loads(row[0]) if row else None


def recent():
    with connect() as db:
        return [
            json.loads(r[0])
            for r in db.execute(
                "SELECT payload FROM runs ORDER BY created DESC LIMIT 100"
            )
        ]


============================================================
FILE: ./backend/services/f1_access.py
============================================================
"""FastF1 3.9 archive access, non-interactive auth and cache preservation.

FastF1 owns login and tokens. The small internal-auth bridge is isolated here
because FastF1 does not yet expose a public non-interactive server API.
"""

from contextlib import contextmanager
from contextvars import ContextVar
from pathlib import Path
import logging
import os
import shutil
import threading

import fastf1
from packaging.version import Version, InvalidVersion

from .loading_errors import SessionDataError

MIN_VERSION = "3.9.2"
LOGIN_COMMAND = "python -m fastf1 auth f1tv --authenticate"
CACHE_ONLY = ContextVar("f1_archive_cache_only", default=False)
LOG = logging.getLogger(__name__)
AUTH_LOCK = threading.RLock()
_TOKEN_STAMP = None


def check_version():
    try:
        supported = Version(fastf1.__version__) >= Version(MIN_VERSION)
    except InvalidVersion:
        supported = False
    if not supported:
        raise SessionDataError(
            "fastf1_upgrade_required",
            f"FastF1 {fastf1.__version__} cannot load the current timing archive correctly. Install backend/requirements.txt (FastF1 >= {MIN_VERSION}) and restart the backend.",
        )


def configure_cache(root, data_dir):
    configured = os.environ.get("F1_CACHE_DIR")
    candidates = [
        data_dir / "fastf1",
        root / "f1_cache",
        root / "formula1-prediction" / "f1_cache",
        root / "formula1-ghost-car" / ".fastf1_cache",
        root / "formula1-best-car" / "cache",
    ]
    populated = (
        lambda path: path.is_dir() and next(path.rglob("*.ff1pkl"), None) is not None
    )
    target = (
        Path(configured).expanduser().resolve()
        if configured
        else next((p for p in candidates if populated(p)), candidates[0])
    )
    target.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    # Preserve all existing parsed caches. Never overwrite, delete or ignore versions.
    for old in candidates:
        if not old.is_dir() or old.resolve() == target.resolve():
            continue
        for source in old.rglob("*.ff1pkl"):
            if source.is_symlink() or target.resolve() in source.resolve().parents:
                continue
            dest = target / source.relative_to(old)
            if dest.exists():
                continue
            dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
            try:
                with source.open("rb") as src, dest.open("xb") as out:
                    shutil.copyfileobj(src, out)
            except FileExistsError:
                pass
    if hasattr(fastf1.Cache, "configure"):
        fastf1.Cache.configure(cache_dir=str(target))
    else:
        fastf1.Cache.enable_cache(str(target))
    return target


def has_cached_timing(session, directory):
    """Avoid running every parser against an entirely cold cache."""
    path = getattr(session, "api_path", "")
    if not path.startswith("/static/"):
        return False
    parsed = directory / path.removeprefix("/static/") / "_extended_timing_data.ff1pkl"
    if parsed.is_file():
        return True
    # Cache 3.9 exposes no public cache-only existence query. Use the underlying
    # requests-cache's public contains() API without making a network request.
    client = getattr(fastf1.Cache, "_requests_session_cached", None)
    if client is not None:
        try:
            return any(
                client.cache.contains(url=f"{host}{path}TimingData.jsonStream")
                for host in (
                    "https://livetiming.formula1.com",
                    "https://livetiming-mirror.fastf1.dev",
                )
            )
        except Exception:
            return False
    return False


def _auth_module():
    from fastf1.internals import f1account

    if not hasattr(f1account.token_manager, "get_stored"):
        raise SessionDataError(
            "auth_integration_unavailable",
            "FastF1 authentication interface changed. Install the supported version from backend/requirements.txt.",
        )
    return f1account


def auth_status():
    """Status only. Never return credentials, claims, JWTs or account details."""
    try:
        module = _auth_module()
        global _TOKEN_STAMP
        manager = module.token_manager
        path = getattr(manager, "_token_path", None)
        if path is not None:
            try:
                info = path.stat()
                stamp = (info.st_mtime_ns, info.st_size)
            except OSError:
                stamp = None
            with AUTH_LOCK:
                if stamp != _TOKEN_STAMP:
                    manager._token = None
                    _TOKEN_STAMP = stamp
        token = manager.get_stored()
        return {
            "authenticated": token is not None and token.is_valid(),
            "credentialsConfigured": bool(
                os.environ.get("F1_USERNAME") and os.environ.get("F1_PASSWORD")
            ),
            "loginCommand": LOGIN_COMMAND,
        }
    except (ImportError, AttributeError, SessionDataError):
        return {
            "authenticated": False,
            "credentialsConfigured": False,
            "loginCommand": LOGIN_COMMAND,
        }


def install_server_policy():
    # The flag is local to this execution context. Other threads' standings and
    # calendar requests are not temporarily switched into offline mode.
    if not getattr(fastf1.Cache.requests_get, "_dashboard_policy", False):
        original = fastf1.Cache.requests_get

        def scoped_get(cls, url, **kwargs):
            if CACHE_ONLY.get():
                kwargs["only_if_cached"] = True
            kwargs.setdefault("timeout", (5, 20))
            return original(url, **kwargs)

        scoped_get._dashboard_policy = True
        fastf1.Cache.requests_get = classmethod(scoped_get)
    try:
        module = _auth_module()
    except (ImportError, SessionDataError):
        return

    def server_login():
        from fastf1.exceptions import F1AuthError

        if (
            module.get_auth_method() == "password"
            and os.environ.get("F1_USERNAME")
            and os.environ.get("F1_PASSWORD")
        ):
            from fastf1.internals.f1account.login import default_login

            return default_login()
        raise F1AuthError(
            f"Backend requests do not prompt for credentials. Run {LOGIN_COMMAND} in a terminal, then retry."
        )

    module.token_manager._default_auth_func = server_login


@contextmanager
def cache_only():
    token = CACHE_ONLY.set(True)
    try:
        yield
    finally:
        CACHE_ONLY.reset(token)


def ensure_authenticated():
    check_version()
    state = auth_status()
    if state["authenticated"]:
        return
    if state["credentialsConfigured"]:
        module = _auth_module()
        with AUTH_LOCK:
            try:
                module.token_manager.get()
                return
            except Exception as exc:
                # Do not expose OAuth response bodies or credentials in API errors.
                raise SessionDataError(
                    "archive_auth_failed",
                    f"F1TV sign-in failed. Authenticate in a terminal with {LOGIN_COMMAND}; an active Access/Pro/Premium subscription is required.",
                ) from exc
    raise SessionDataError(
        "archive_auth_required",
        f"Uncached F1 lap timing, weather and telemetry require F1TV authentication. Run {LOGIN_COMMAND} with an active Access/Pro/Premium account, then retry. Standings and published qualifying/race results remain available without this login.",
    )


def probe_archive(session):
    """Probe one real archive resource using FastF1's authenticated transport."""
    url = f"https://livetiming.formula1.com{session.api_path}SessionInfo.jsonStream"
    try:
        response = fastf1.Cache.requests_get(url, timeout=(5, 15))
    except Exception as exc:
        raise SessionDataError(
            "archive_network_error",
            "The F1 timing archive could not be reached. Check the backend network connection and retry.",
            502,
            15,
        ) from exc
    code = response.status_code
    if code == 401:
        raise SessionDataError(
            "archive_auth_rejected",
            f"The F1 archive rejected authentication. Run {LOGIN_COMMAND} again and verify the account subscription.",
        )
    if code == 403:
        raise SessionDataError(
            "archive_access_denied",
            "The F1 archive refused access (HTTP 403). Verify the F1TV account entitlement and network access to livetiming.formula1.com.",
        )
    if code == 404:
        raise SessionDataError(
            "session_not_published",
            "This session's archive data is not published at the expected path yet. Retry later.",
            425,
            60,
        )
    if code == 429:
        raise SessionDataError(
            "archive_rate_limited",
            "The F1 timing provider is rate limiting requests. Retry shortly.",
            503,
            60,
        )
    if code != 200:
        raise SessionDataError(
            "archive_upstream_error",
            f"The F1 archive returned HTTP {code}; retry later.",
            502,
            30,
        )


============================================================
FILE: ./backend/services/weekend.py
============================================================
"""Descriptive comparisons, without named-driver bonuses or causal claims."""

from itertools import combinations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from .data import clean_laps, iso, load_session, number, seconds, optional_data
from .loading_errors import SessionDataError

LIMITS = [
    "Same team does not guarantee identical upgrades, setup, damage or engine modes.",
    "Fuel mass, setup, tyre carcass temperatures and physical tyre wear are not public timing channels.",
    "Same-lap matching reduces fuel-burn and weather timing differences; fuel loads can still differ.",
    "Traffic, management, damage and strategy can remain in green-flag laps. Results are associations, not driver-skill scores.",
    "Compound labels are weekend-relative; SOFT at different circuits need not be the same Pirelli specification.",
]


def matched_laps(laps, first, second, age_tolerance=1):
    clean = clean_laps(laps)
    columns = ["LapNumber", "Compound", "TyreLife", "seconds", "Stint"]
    if not set(columns).issubset(clean.columns):
        return pd.DataFrame()
    a = clean.loc[clean["Driver"].eq(first), columns].dropna()
    b = clean.loc[clean["Driver"].eq(second), columns].dropna()
    both = a.merge(
        b, on=["LapNumber", "Compound"], suffixes=("A", "B"), validate="one_to_one"
    )
    both = both.loc[
        (both["TyreLifeA"] - both["TyreLifeB"]).abs() <= age_tolerance
    ].copy()
    both["delta"] = both["secondsA"] - both["secondsB"]
    return both.sort_values("LapNumber")


def interval(pairs):
    """Moving-block bootstrap within contiguous matched stint-pair segments."""
    if len(pairs) < 8:
        return None
    groups, current, previous = [], [], None
    for _, row in pairs.iterrows():
        key = (row["Compound"], row["StintA"], row["StintB"])
        if previous is not None and (
            key != previous[0] or row["LapNumber"] != previous[1] + 1
        ):
            groups.append(np.array(current))
            current = []
        current.append(row["delta"])
        previous = (key, row["LapNumber"])
    if current:
        groups.append(np.array(current))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(17)
    values = []
    for _ in range(600):
        draw = []
        for group in groups:
            starts = rng.integers(0, len(group), size=(len(group) + 2) // 3)
            block = np.concatenate(
                [group[(np.arange(3) + s) % len(group)] for s in starts]
            )[: len(group)]
            draw.extend(block)
        values.append(np.median(draw))
    return [number(x) for x in np.quantile(values, [0.025, 0.975])]


def published_qualifying_gap(session, first, second):
    rows = session.results.set_index("Abbreviation")
    if first in rows.index and second in rows.index:
        for column in ("Q3", "Q2", "Q1"):
            a, b = rows.loc[first].get(column), rows.loc[second].get(column)
            if pd.notna(a) and pd.notna(b):
                return {
                    "segment": column,
                    "deltaSeconds": number((a - b).total_seconds()),
                    "firstSeconds": seconds(a),
                    "secondSeconds": seconds(b),
                    "reason": "Published Jolpica qualifying classification, deepest shared timed segment. This comparison uses classified times, not raw lap/flag checks.",
                }
    return {
        "segment": None,
        "deltaSeconds": None,
        "reason": "No shared timed qualifying segment in the published classification",
    }


def qualifying_gap(session, first, second):
    if getattr(session, "published_results", False) or getattr(
        session, "qualifying_classification", False
    ):
        return published_qualifying_gap(session, first, second)
    if getattr(session, "data_provider", None) == "openf1":
        return {
            "segment": None,
            "deltaSeconds": None,
            "reason": "Shared qualifying segments require a published qualifying classification; segments are not guessed from lap times.",
        }
    try:
        stages = session.laps.split_qualifying_sessions()
    except (ValueError, AttributeError):
        return {
            "segment": None,
            "deltaSeconds": None,
            "reason": "Qualifying segments unavailable",
        }
    for index in (2, 1, 0):
        if stages[index] is None:
            continue
        clean = clean_laps(stages[index])
        a = clean.loc[clean["Driver"].eq(first), "seconds"]
        b = clean.loc[clean["Driver"].eq(second), "seconds"]
        if len(a) and len(b):
            return {
                "segment": f"Q{index+1}",
                "deltaSeconds": number(a.min() - b.min()),
                "firstSeconds": number(a.min()),
                "secondSeconds": number(b.min()),
                "reason": "Fastest valid laps in deepest shared segment; track evolution remains a confounder",
            }
    return {
        "segment": None,
        "deltaSeconds": None,
        "reason": "No shared segment with valid timed laps",
    }


def degradation(laps, driver):
    """Fit lap time ~ race lap + tyre age per compound only if identifiable."""
    clean = clean_laps(laps)
    result = []
    for compound, df in clean.loc[clean["Driver"].eq(driver)].groupby("Compound"):
        df = df.dropna(subset=["TyreLife", "LapNumber", "Stint", "seconds"])
        item = {
            "compound": str(compound),
            "laps": len(df),
            "slopeSecondsPerTyreLap": None,
            "reason": "Requires 15 valid laps and two stints on this compound",
        }
        if len(df) >= 15 and df["Stint"].nunique() >= 2:
            x = df[["LapNumber", "TyreLife"]].to_numpy(dtype=float)
            scale = x.std(axis=0)
            if (scale == 0).any():
                result.append(item)
                continue
            z = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(df)), (x - x.mean(axis=0)) / scale])
            if np.linalg.matrix_rank(z) < 3 or np.linalg.cond(z) > 30:
                item["reason"] = (
                    "Tyre age and race lap cannot be separated in this sample"
                )
            else:
                fit = np.linalg.lstsq(z, df["seconds"].to_numpy(), rcond=None)[0]
                item.update(
                    slopeSecondsPerTyreLap=number(fit[2] / scale[1]),
                    reason="Adjusted lap-time trend, not measured physical tyre wear; traffic, weather and stint effects remain",
                )
        result.append(item)
    return result


def field_pace(race, qualifying):
    """Observed peer-relative pace; peer sets vary, so this is not a car ranking."""
    clean = clean_laps(race.laps).dropna(subset=["TyreLife", "Compound"])
    samples = {}
    for _, group in clean.groupby(["LapNumber", "Compound"]):
        for _, lap in group.iterrows():
            peers = group.loc[
                group["Driver"].ne(lap["Driver"])
                & (group["TyreLife"] - lap["TyreLife"]).abs().le(1)
            ]
            if peers["Driver"].nunique() < 2:
                continue
            benchmark = peers["seconds"].median()
            samples.setdefault(str(lap["Driver"]), []).append(
                100 * (lap["seconds"] / benchmark - 1)
            )
    q_positions = (
        {}
        if qualifying is None
        else {
            str(row["Abbreviation"]): number(row["Position"], 0)
            for _, row in qualifying.results.iterrows()
        }
    )
    result = []
    for _, row in race.results.iterrows():
        driver = str(row["Abbreviation"])
        values = samples.get(driver, [])
        result.append(
            {
                "driver": driver,
                "team": str(row["TeamName"]),
                "qualifyingPosition": q_positions.get(driver),
                "finishPosition": number(row["Position"], 0),
                "matchedLaps": len(values),
                "relativePacePercent": (
                    number(np.median(values)) if len(values) >= 5 else None
                ),
            }
        )
    return result


def weekend(year, round_number):
    sessions, errors, failures = {}, [], []
    for kind in ("Q", "R"):
        try:
            sessions[kind] = load_session(year, round_number, kind)
        except SessionDataError as exc:
            failures.append(exc)
            errors.append(f"{kind} raw timing unavailable: {exc.detail}")
            if kind == "Q":
                try:
                    sessions[kind] = load_session(
                        year, round_number, kind, results_only=True
                    )
                    errors.append(
                        "Qualifying comparison uses published results; raw tyre/flag checks are unavailable."
                    )
                except SessionDataError as fallback:
                    failures.append(fallback)
        except Exception:
            errors.append(f"{kind} timing data unavailable or not published yet")
    if not sessions:
        if failures:
            raise failures[0]
        raise SessionDataError(
            "weekend_data_unavailable",
            "Qualifying and race data could not be loaded. Run python -m backend.check_data with --timing.",
            502,
        )
    for kind, session in sessions.items():
        errors.extend(f"{kind}: {w}" for w in getattr(session, "data_warnings", []))
    source = sessions.get("R") or sessions.get("Q")
    race = sessions.get("R")
    quali = sessions.get("Q")
    roster = {}
    for session in sessions.values():
        for _, row in session.results.iterrows():
            team, driver = str(row["TeamName"]), str(row["Abbreviation"])
            if driver and driver != "nan":
                roster.setdefault(team, set()).add(driver)
    teams = []
    for team, drivers in sorted(roster.items()):
        for first, second in combinations(sorted(drivers), 2):
            pairs = (
                matched_laps(race.laps, first, second)
                if race is not None
                else pd.DataFrame()
            )
            q = (
                qualifying_gap(quali, first, second)
                if quali is not None
                else {
                    "segment": None,
                    "deltaSeconds": None,
                    "reason": "Qualifying unavailable",
                }
            )
            delta = number(pairs["delta"].median()) if len(pairs) else None
            ci = interval(pairs) if len(pairs) else None
            reversal = (
                q["deltaSeconds"] is not None
                and delta is not None
                and q["deltaSeconds"] * delta < 0
            )
            teams.append(
                {
                    "team": team,
                    "first": first,
                    "second": second,
                    "qualifying": q,
                    "raceDeltaSeconds": delta,
                    "matchedLaps": len(pairs),
                    "interval95": ci,
                    "paceReversal": bool(reversal),
                    "samples": [
                        {
                            "lap": int(r.LapNumber),
                            "first": number(r.secondsA),
                            "second": number(r.secondsB),
                            "delta": number(r.delta),
                            "compound": str(r.Compound),
                            "ageA": int(r.TyreLifeA),
                            "ageB": int(r.TyreLifeB),
                        }
                        for r in pairs.itertuples()
                    ],
                    "tyreTrends": {
                        d: degradation(race.laps, d) if race is not None else []
                        for d in (first, second)
                    },
                }
            )
    weather = None
    wd = optional_data(source, "weather_data")
    if wd is not None and not wd.empty:
        weather = {
            key: number(wd[key].mean())
            for key in ("AirTemp", "TrackTemp", "Humidity", "WindSpeed")
        }
        weather["rainfall"] = bool(wd["Rainfall"].dropna().gt(0).any())
    return {
        "year": year,
        "round": round_number,
        "eventName": str(source.event["EventName"]),
        "teams": teams,
        "weather": weather,
        "fieldPace": field_pace(race, quali) if race is not None else [],
        "warnings": errors,
        "dataSources": {
            kind: getattr(value, "data_source", "FastF1 timing archive / cache")
            for kind, value in sessions.items()
        },
        "limitations": LIMITS,
        "method": "A minus B; negative means A faster. Race pairs: same lap, same compound, tyre age within one lap. FastF1 samples require accurate, non-deleted, non-generated, green-flag and no pit laps; OpenF1 samples use the distinct conservative sector/pit/race-control screening described in the source warnings. Exploratory 95% block-bootstrap interval needs 8 pairs.",
        "unavailable": [
            "Fuel mass (kg)",
            "Setup settings",
            "Physical tyre wear",
            "Brake pressure",
            "Aero loads",
        ],
    }

FILE: ./backend/services/events.py
